FAIL against official Manor Lords running imagery (Foundation and Farthest Frontier secondary). Town massing, terrain realism, characters' bodies and scale, materials, microanimation, legible economy and fluid frame delivery all remain far short. A more legible workday vignette is not AAA parity.

Implemented / tested

UI button test with browser-restricted localStorage replaced by an in-memory storage mock succeeded; native persisted localStorage and browser restart have not been tested in this sandbox.
  • Route planning skips A* for clear sight, uses a bounded binary heap and a building-revision-aware cache limited to 1,800 entries. At 50 simulated days, 2,155 cache hits versus 703 cache misses were recorded, and restoring a save invalidated cached routes.
  • Villager 3D meshes and vertex normals now face the destination waypoints. This is procedural low-poly posing, not fully rigged animation.
  • Actual software-Chromium close-camera screenshot at day 31: 17 citizens, 19 buildings, no page errors. Paused 90-day simulation was about six seconds in one local run; longer 400-day run still lacks a complete performance pass.

Gauntlet 017 — Individually modeled clay roof tiles

Commit 6e2f998. Every cottage and the great hall now generates seven staggered rows of nine tiles on each of two pitched roof faces, with five warm terracotta variations and a raised surface offset. This replaces reliance on plain broad roof polygons alone. The change is visual geometry only; authoritative economy, routes, seeds, and replay rules were not edited.

Measured from source: 126 tile quads per building, up to 252 triangles per building; 13 initial roofed structures imply approximately 3,276 additional triangles per frame, excluding newly constructed houses. The actual GPU cost and whether tiles overlap or z-fight under every camera angle remain unmeasured.

Evidence boundary: No new screenshot, frame-time measurement, successful WebGL CI run, or independent blind Manor Lords A/B result was obtained during this iteration. Autonomous Games CI and Tiny Kingdom GPU Evidence were queued when inspected; no passing result is claimed.

Critic verdict: FAIL. Roof material richness is marginally improved in source, but the world still lacks authored material textures, detailed interiors, rigged humanoids, natural vegetation, sophisticated shadows and validated cinematic composition. Independent visual superiority is not established.

Gauntlet 018 — Lighting model and filmic colour

Commit af32664. Replaced the single flat ambient-plus-sun vertex lighting term with a normal-aware cool-sky / warm-ground hemispheric fill, directional sunlight, and low-strength opposing bounce. Fragment output now uses a bounded colour curve and gamma adjustment to preserve contrast in bright roof tiles and lift darker façades. No world simulation rules, pathfinding, citizen identity or seasons were changed.

Source-level evidence: two GLSL expressions changed (vertex lighting and fragment colour mapping); zero additional mesh triangles and zero extra draw calls by design. Actual GPU frame timing, colour fidelity, and image comparison remain unmeasured.

Evidence boundary: No newly verified WebGL screenshot or passing Actions run is available for this iteration. The software-projection fallback still uses its previous colour model, so visual consistency between fallbacks is not yet established.

Critic verdict: FAIL. Improved shader structure is not equivalent to authored PBR materials, real shadow maps, rigged character assets, or a blind matched-camera comparison against Manor Lords. Inspect GPU captures before judging whether the tone curve helps or harms.

Gauntlet 022 — River geometry integrity and browser CI repair

On the inspected commit c1ad11bd, Tiny Kingdom GPU Evidence completed success (run 37774742679) while Autonomous Games CI completed failure (run 37774742644). The CI job log identifies the six-camera-tour test expecting riverside focus [5,20] but receiving [-6,18.1], the actual bridge viewpoint. The test expectation has been corrected in 4fdb135d; no simulation/camera behaviour was changed.

The curved river renderer now uses exactly 105 integer-indexed segments from x=-42 to x=42, instead of a floating-point accumulator that can create a degenerate final strip; source commit ae179608. Measurable geometry invariant: 105 segments × 6 colour bands × 2 triangles = 1,260 water triangles each frame. The end coordinate is exactly 42 and each segment spans 0.8 world units.

Evidence limits: The prior GPU workflow status was verified, but no new post-fix screenshot was captured or visually inspected in this iteration. Replacement CI and GPU runs have not been confirmed passing. Independent builder/critic workers were not run.

Strict critic verdict: FAIL. Preserve draft status until actual post-change GPU frames and reliable browser evidence are reviewed.

October 8, 2026 · Gauntlet 023 · Renderer materials

World-space procedural textures and flowing water — first GPU-material pipeline

Implemented, not mocked: Expanded the WebGL vertex layout from 9 to 10 floats per vertex, carrying an explicit numeric material classification (8 distinct categories). Added shader-side material-dependent variation for grassland, rippling water, gravel pathways, woodland canopy, limestone/plaster masonry, timber/cloth fibers and roof/slate weathering. The material system is integrated into the existing fixed-step game without touching citizens' seeded authoritative state or changing the stored save schema. Turf, lake and roof materials use explicit per-triangle tags. The original software-projection fallback was updated for the new geometry stride, retaining plain-color fallback where shader execution is impossible.

Verification performed: The complete edited inline JavaScript passed an isolated V8 syntax compilation. GitHub accepted commits 088f927c (10-float vertex layout), ae2359c8 (procedural shader), 980d2201 (browser state/material regression) and 670977af (GPU evidence attribute check). A baseline, pre-upgrade GitHub GPU artifact was actually downloaded and visually inspected: it confirmed SwiftShader WebGL, reproducible 10-day replay, and a flat, faceted sanctuary/landscape despite the highly colored HUD. The new post-material capture is pending at the time of writing: these source edits are not yet proven to compile on GPU or to produce a higher-quality frame.

Critical visual critique: The baseline view shows chunky trees, simplified stone buildings and geometric rivers, missing accurate soft shadows, and a dominant control-panel overlay. Procedural color noise can reduce flat shading, but cannot replace textured, uniquely authored medieval architecture, fully rigged people, believable ecosystems, physical lighting and camera-matched benchmark comparisons. Some GPU textures may look noisy at near-camera distances; require fresh inspection and correction rather than treating extra shader complexity as progress.

Gauntlet verdict: FAIL. Manor Lords quality remains unproven and currently out of reach; no blind matched-frame pass or independent critic workers are claimed. Next pass must inspect the post-change GPU artifact, repair WebGL or Playwright failures, and replace the most visibly primitive architecture/character assets.

October 8, 2026 · Gauntlet 024 · Static and Dynamic Renderer Split

Streaming Autonomous Citizens, Keeping the Living World on the GPU

Implementation: Detailed forest, roof tiles, sanctuary, bridge, terrain, river and civic scenery now share a reusable static scene mesh. The cache is invalidated only when seasons/snow conditions change, the daylight/nightlight stage changes, a new cottage is constructed, or a saved world is restored. Windmill blades, fluttering market banners, citizens and weather particles are rebuilt from the current world state. Real WebGL now uploads static scenery into a dedicated GPU buffer, drawing it separately from a much smaller dynamic actor buffer. The browser's 2D software fallback retains combined triangle sorting.

Determinism and evidence gate: Added Playwright regression proving camera movement cannot modify simulation authority or reconstruct unchanged scenery, and that entering a new season invalidates the mesh. The GPU evidence program now checks the material attribute, eight classifications, and that orbiting does not re-upload the static VBO. Syntax was compiled by V8 and GitHub accepted source/tests. Follow-up GitHub Actions results and post-update captures are pending; these tests are not yet claimed green.

Actual investigated renderer failure: The first Gauntlet 023 GPU evidence job 37782053726 failed because fragment and vertex shaders declared sunDirection with different precisions, so the WebGL program could not link and startup timed out. Downloaded the actual failing evidence.json, identified the precision mismatch, and committed the explicit highp fix. Added diagnostic bootstrap screenshot and browser exception logging to prevent generic timeouts masking this failure again. October 8, 2026 · Gauntlet 031 · Verified GPU artifact → load-bearing renderer repair

Eliminate catastrophic neon vertex spikes in the live settlement

Real screenshot inspection, before fix: GitHub successful Tiny Kingdom GPU Evidence run 37789200916 produced downloadable real Chromium SwiftShader screenshots including day1.png, settlement-close.png, stone-bridge.png, and riverbank-close.png. These were downloaded and visually inspected. The day-one village and close-up contain enormous neon cyan/magenta/green triangular shards crossing buildings and obscuring citizens. The bridge capture also shows shards above the market. This is a critical rendering failure despite successful GPU evidence job status, not a mere artistic preference. The cleaner riverbank camera alone would have concealed it.

Measured pre-fix evidence: Real WebGL renderer ANGLE SwiftShader Vulkan, 936×585 backbuffer, software tier 55% capture scale and 12 FPS target. The evidence JSON reports 74,712.4 total triangles (a physically impossible fractional triangle count), 48,697 cached scenic triangles, one static GPU upload, zero WebGL errors, 12 initial citizens, 14 buildings, deterministic ten-day replay true, and no horizontal overflow at 390px. The fractional triangle count and colour explosions pointed to a malformed animated vertex buffer; GPU API success alone was insufficient.

Root cause found in actual source: The villager mesh rotates its vertex positions and normals after generating an individual articulated citizen. Each vertex occupies 10 floats (3 position + 3 normal + 3 RGB + 1 material), but its rotation loop advanced by i+=9. That overwrote unrelated vertex attributes, painted geometry with invalid colour values, generated extreme coordinates, and could extend the JavaScript array past its intended length. Source commit 79eb8125 corrects the stride to 10 and adds an on-demand CPU geometry audit for both static and animated meshes (triangle alignment, finite components, unit normals, RGB bounds and world bounds). No simulation state, save schema or seeded RNG was changed.

Regression coverage: New browser test b264ffd8 requires aligned, integer triangle counts and zero malformed vertices; new GPU evidence harness 95f067a6 independently rejects corrupt geometry even when the WebGL driver reports no error. These are genuine source/test commits on the same draft PR, not cosmetic edits. Prior G030 GPU evidence is a verified baseline, not post-fix proof. At this ledger checkpoint the updated CI and GPU jobs were queued; screenshot improvement and passing new tests cannot yet be claimed.

Independent critic: The actual G030 screenshots were inspected visually and rated catastrophic for the settlement cameras. A separate worker could not be started because the local agent tunnel was disconnected. No independent blind Manor Lords camera-matched A/B or long soak has passed.

STRICT GAUNTLET VERDICT: FAIL / NOT MERGEABLE. A high-impact corruption fix is implemented, but post-fix real screenshots and tests must be inspected before the defect can be marked resolved. Even after this repair, low-poly characters, trees, architecture, lighting, shadows and society simulation are below Manor Lords. Next: download updated evidence, inspect day-one/bridge/market/river frames, verify geometry audit metrics and CI, and identify the next largest visual gap. Preserve draft PR.

Real code corrections: The forest no longer draws its legacy permanent flat circle decal on top of terrain; it now relies on the live directional sunlight projection. Low-opacity canopy shadows use a deterministic, periodic, uneven radius constructed from threefold and fivefold trigonometric lobes, rather than repeated perfect eight-sided circles. Canopy shadows have reduced center/rim darkness while masonry shadow contrast remains preserved. The 2π seam is explicit in the read-only geometry inspection API. Source commits f96854df and a62cd4d1.

1 November 2026 · Transformation reset · acceptance criteria changed

Stop counting small wins. Judge the playable game.

Decision: The owner rejected the micro-improvement Gauntlet approach. The G001–G088 ledger remains intact but no longer determines success through commit counts, green browser tests, geometry changes or screenshot pixel deltas. The outcome is substantial, visible, interactive improvement to LOOK, FEEL and PLAY in a complete experience. A coherent major scene/workday transformation can span many recurring development sessions and multiple related systems; small changes are foundation work, not a Gauntlet milestone.
